# Church of Elgira

![[Church of Elgira Symbol.png|240]]

The **Church of Elgira** is Perfid's dominant religious institution. Church of the Saviour, Saviour's Church and Faith of Elgira are recognised aliases, though Church of Elgira is the most common name.

## Leadership

- **High Illuminant Derathel** is the head of the Church.
- **Arch-Illuminant Serathiel** holds a separate senior office and signed the recovered instructions sent to Alistair Prusset.

The two titles refer to different people and different positions, not an alias or continuity error.

## Known campaign position

- The Church ordered [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot's]] murder and used [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]] to contact a [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]] cell.
- Its leadership believed, or claimed to believe, that Anthea was **Saint Marra**, one of Elgira's surviving followers, and feared she intended to restore Ardynia.
- In Session 25, the Defracturers and Elmwood witnessed an Ardynian memory; **Elmwood recognised Anthea as Saint Marra**. The party now treats this identification as established. It is **not necessarily public knowledge**, and the Church's precise motives remain a separate question.
- Perfidian agents abducted and tortured [[03 People/Notable Figures/Terrick Lowgins|Terrick Lowgins]].
- Church agents escaped Somnolent with a [[Corona of the Ashen Choir|Corona of the Ashen Choir]].

## Doctrine and history

The Church teaches that Elgira deliberately sacrificed herself at the Battle of the Vale. In Session 25, Elmwood activated the Tace Nox scroll using probable Sanguis Aevi and the Defracturers experienced an ancient memory of the ritual. The memory revealed a catastrophic miscalculation and the deaths of the rebel host, rather than the carefully measured sacrifice of official doctrine. The vision is **party-discovered evidence**, not a public historical consensus. See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 25 - Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages|Session 25]] and [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/The Sundering of Ardynia|The Sundering of Ardynia]].

# Crimson Shield

> [!summary] At a glance
> A polished and highly competent adventuring company led by [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Orrian Leovyre|Orrian Leovyre]], Ballar's childhood friend.

## Members

- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Orrian Leovyre|Orrian Leovyre]] â€” high elf fighter and leader; painfully humble and extremely competent.
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Mera Thorn|Mera Thorn]] â€” half-elf ranger and capable tracker.
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Osric Pell|Osric Pell]] â€” dwarven cleric known for very long stories.
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Veyra Sal|Veyra Sal]] â€” tiefling sorcerer with considerable confidence in her own presentation.
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Tomkin Reed|Tomkin Reed]] â€” halfling rogue, shamelessly charming and quickly popular.

## Session 24

The Crimson Shield arrived at the Defracturers' Bastion on Day Ten of the party's absence after recently dealing with a **spirit naga**. They stayed as guests before continuing toward **Shawwake**.

Bastion staff repeatedly suspected that the group, and Orrian in particular, were too polished to be genuine. Their attempts to uncover a hidden motive instead continued to support the simpler explanation that the Crimson Shield were exactly what they appeared to be.

During their stay, the group surprised two men unloading a boat beside the river. The smugglers fled and abandoned barrels containing expensive goods with stripped or obscured merchant markings. Orrian brought one barrel back to the Bastion, where it was turned over to Alaaric Hammerfall and later connected to Antoinne Cassan.

Before leaving, Orrian entrusted Ghorgadush with a small parcel for Ballar.

## Session 25 â€” reputation, gift and Phoenix Yard

Upon returning to Somnolent, the Defracturers discovered that the Crimson Shield had become conspicuously popular. Elmwood reported that the company had dealt with remaining trouble near **Tace Nox**, although no detailed account of that encounter is recorded.

A sign at the burned [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/One Ball Inn|One Ball Inn]] announced the planned **Phoenix Yard**, generously supported by Orrian Leovyre and the Crimson Shield; the development is not yet complete.

The parcel Orrian left for Ballar contained a **Cloak of Many Fashions** and an affectionate, gently patronising letter inviting him to meet Orrian in **Temir**. The gift intensified Ballar's irritation at the party having stayed in his Bastion. See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 25 - Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages|Session 25]].

## Session 26 â€” reported travel to Temir

During a visit to the Doro embassy, Ballar heard that the Crimson Shield were heading towards **Temir** for the coming **Wavecutter tournament**. This is a reported itinerary, not confirmation of their arrival or participation. Ballar hopes to catch up with [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Orrian Leovyre|Orrian Leovyre]]. See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 26 - Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl|Session 26]].

additional_source: Session 25
updated: 2026-10-13

# Loyal Crows

The **Loyal Crows** were Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s hidden agents and confidants. Their identities and structure were deliberately concealed, leaving the party to distinguish genuine loyalists from those merely invoking the name.

## Known members and claims

- [[Eden Carrion]] is identified as the Loyal Crow who hired [[Buggie]] to abduct Alistair Prusset.
- [[Summer Quaresh]] is recorded as another of Antheaâ€™s Crows.
- Antheaâ€™s note from Tace Nox says three Crows had already died and that only Anthea and Eden remained at that time.
- The groupâ€™s current structure, resources and surviving membership are unknown.

> [!question] Unresolved timeline
> Anthea's note reports that three Crows had died and only Anthea and Eden remained, yet Summer Quaresh is separately identified as a Crow. It is unknown whether Summer joined later, used the title differently, or whether another part of the timeline remains unaccounted for.

## Documents

- [[Loyal Crow's Orders to Buggie]]
- [[Anthea Bloodclot's Note on Tace Nox]]

## Search for Eden and Summer

During the Defracturers' absence, the Bastion became a temporary base for renewed efforts to locate [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eden Carrion|Eden Carrion]] and [[03 People/Notable Figures/Summer Quaresh|Summer Quaresh]].

[[03 People/Bastion Staff/Caelen Briar|Caelen Briar]] attempted sympathetic magic using possessions associated with them but received only magical static. A reported sighting was later matched by [[03 People/Bastion Staff/Gudren Flint|Gudren Flint]] and Caelen to an area described in old records as naturally dampening magic. The lead was strong enough for Job Ashmere and Gayle Bramble to prepare to leave Somnolent in pursuit.

The sighting has not yet been confirmed, and neither missing Crow has been recovered.

## Session 25 â€” additional leads

Gudren and Caelen briefed the returned Defracturers on three research trails:

- **[[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Tessa Betu|Tessa Betu]]:** an Erzhin stonecutter associated with ancient laws and the Stone Chronicles, reportedly met Eden Carrion around eighteen months previously. A purported meeting involving Father Zantos was not recalled by Zantos.
- **[[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Arun Yev|Arun Yev]]:** a Temiri historical scholar who met Summer Quaresh repeatedly. After a short unexplained absence following Anthea's murder he rose rapidly through the Wavecutters; talk of future clan leadership remains rumour.
- **[[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Galt|Galt]] and [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eula|Eula]]:** former Rhozka worshippers reportedly expelled from Voruun for necromancy; records indicate multiple meetings with both Eden and Summer.

These are **research leads, not confirmed members**. The missing Crows' whereabouts and the faction's full membership remain unresolved.

See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 25 - Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages|Session 25]].

# The Rootbound

> *â€œBreak the roots, free the forest.â€*

| | |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Dissent within [[Clan Orvak]] |
| **Structure** | Independent cells with no accepted central command |
| **Symbol** | An inverted evergreen with chained roots |
| **Shared cause** | Resistance to imposed hierarchy and outside control |

## Symbol

![[Rootbound Sigil.png]]

The Rootbound use an **inverted evergreen whose roots terminate in chains**.

- The evergreen recalls Orvak's endurance.
- Its inversion rejects the idea that tradition must always be obeyed.
- The chains represent obligations to chiefs, foreign patrons and distant rulers.

The mark is carved into wood, stone and sometimes flesh. Depending on the cell, it may be a warning, a threat or a rallying sign.

## Origins

The movement emerged as Orvaki communities were pressed to accept stronger tribal leadership and greater participation in Anthea Bloodclot's project of clan unity. Many Orvaki considered this a practical response to a changing world. The Rootbound regarded it as the surrender of local autonomy.

Their central argument was simple: Orvak drew its strength from small, resilient communities bound by kinship and custom, not from obedience to a distant chieftain, a foreign patron or a self-appointed Lord of the Five Clans.

Anthea became the movement's defining enemy. Rootbound songs described her as a **â€œpoisoned seedâ€**, an outsider whose alliances would reshape Orvak until little of its independence remained.

## One name, many movements

The Rootbound's contradictions are not separate discarded versions of the organisation. They are a consequence of its design.

Cells act independently and often pursue rival ambitions. They share symbols, coded songs and a broad hostility to imposed authority, but no leader can enforce a single interpretation of the creed. The movement's decentralisation protects it from destruction while repeatedly undermining its own message.

Some cells are **radical decentralists**. They welcome outsiders who accept local autonomy and imagine a network of self-governing communities linked by custom rather than law.

Other cells are **isolationist and exclusionary**, presenting themselves as guardians of Orvaki purity. They treat trade, migration and foreign alliances as cultural erosion.

Still others are primarily violent opportunists who use Rootbound language to justify sabotage, private vendettas or paid work. Two cells may use the same symbol while considering one another traitors.

## Methods

Actions attributed to Rootbound cells include:

- Sabotage of trade routes and foreign supply lines.
- Threats against leaders judged to be compromised.
- Poisoning and targeted assassination.
- Attacks on caravans, officials and political intermediaries.
- Use of fear to make cooperation with outsiders costly.

Because responsibility is deliberately fragmented, an act committed by one cell does not necessarily reflect the plans or approval of another.

## Anthea Bloodclot

The Rootbound have taken **quiet credit** for Anthea's death through private boasts, songs and coded claims, but the movement has never issued an open confirmation. Some cells call the killing an act of purification; others deny responsibility while celebrating the result.

The public case remains uncertain. The party's investigation has gone further.

> [!info] What the Defracturers uncovered
> The Defracturers found evidence that Perfid, acting through [[Alistair Prusset]], hired a Rootbound cell to kill Anthea Bloodclot. They have also directly observed Rootbound agents cooperating with Perfidian interests and found a Rootbound insignia among those responsible for the destruction of Buggie's gang.

## Perfid and the Rootbound

Perfid funds, shields or employs parts of the movement when convenient. This is a pragmatic relationship rather than a true ideological alliance. Rootbound rhetoric condemns foreign interference, yet individual cells accept foreign coin and protection when it advances their own goals.

That hypocrisy is not hidden by a central leadership because no such leadership exists. One cell may work with Perfid while another considers that cooperation proof of betrayal.

# The Defracturers

The adventurers were informally called the **Squishy Boys** during their earliest exploits. They formally adopted the name **The Defracturers** when they accepted [[03 People/Notable Figures/Romayne Drayton|Romayne DraytonÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s]] patronage in Session 11.

## Members

- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]] Ã¢â‚¬â€ high-elf paladin connected to Doro and House Nirrell
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Erelmor|Erelmor]] Ã¢â‚¬â€ drow rogue pursuing those responsible for the destruction of his family
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]] Ã¢â‚¬â€ wood-elf Circle of Stars druid with ties to Clan Orvak
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Enrak Kiran|Enrak Kiran]] Ã¢â‚¬â€ half-elf fire mage from Clan Khaali
- [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]] Ã¢â‚¬â€ dragonborn wizard and former captive of the Broken Fang

## Formal status

Following Perfid's actions in Somnolent, the group entered formal patronage under Romayne Drayton. The signed [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Instrument of Patronage and Service|Instrument of Patronage & Service]] grants diplomatic protections inside Somnolent, basic staff, a monthly stipend and use of the former Perfid embassy, with significant obligations regarding conduct, assignments and recovered artefacts. These are **contractual rights and duties**, not unrestricted ownership of the Bastion or blanket immunity.

## Household and mounts

- [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/The Defracturers' Bastion|The DefracturersÃ¢â‚¬â„¢ Bastion]]
- [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses|Horses]]
- [[03 People/index#Bastion staff|Bastion staff]]

## Current campaign position

By the end of Session 25 (background carried forward; see the Session 26 status below):

- The party holds the [[Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]] and their heart stone.
- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Mireya Dinak|Mireya Dinak]] remains dead; her preserved body was brought to Elmwood in Somnolent in Session 25 for possible restoration.
- Ballar also preserved the corpse of a Broken Fang scout in Session 22; that body was brought to Elmwood in Session 25. Neither body has been restored.
- n'Dong recovered two vials of [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Dragon's Dandruff|Dragon's Dandruff]] from Talan Rook's tent.
- Ieuan planted the remaining [[Plague|Plague]] cuttings, but Plague's voice continues to reach him.
- The party encountered [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Oakrah Woodfrey|Oakrah Woodfrey]] and destroyed the immediate corruption beneath the home of [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Maelor Vey|Maelor Vey, the False Gardener]].
- Ballar released Malt with instructions to acquire a Pot of Awakening, awaken [[Bushtache|Bushtache]] and bring the cabbage to Somnolent.
- The party deferred Hushvale and **returned to Somnolent in Session 25**.
- During their absence, their Bastion staff uncovered a credible lead on Eden Carrion and Summer Quaresh; Job Ashmere and Gayle Bramble prepared to pursue it, while the One Ball Inn's Broken Fang connections surfaced publicly enough to become dangerous.
- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ervain Megbanas|the Old Grey Pike]] left Somnolent, [[03 People/Notable Figures/Antoinne Cassan|Antoinne Cassan]] disappeared, and the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/One Ball Inn|One Ball Inn]] burned down before the party's return.

## Session 25 - status and discoveries

**Current party level:** **6** (advanced at the end of Session 25).

The Defracturers returned to Somnolent, encountered deadly creatures beneath the burned One Ball and learned new Loyal Crows leads from their Bastion researchers. Elmwood activated the [[06 Library/Ardynian Relics and Artefacts/Tace Nox scroll|Tace Nox scroll]] using likely Sanguis Aevi, and the party witnessed Elgira's memory of the Sundering. They recognised the previously encountered [[06 Library/Ardynian Relics and Artefacts/Corona of the Ashen Choir|Corona of the Ashen Choir]] as one of the five Crowns of Infinity, and the [[06 Library/Ardynian Relics and Artefacts/Staff of Broken Time|Staff of Broken Time]] as the staff Elgira used. Elmwood identified Anthea as Saint Marra.

The group now faces a choice about whether to gather, safeguard or destroy dangerous Ardynian relics. The Bands remain unattuned. The fate of the escaped slaad tadpoles and the meaning of the burned farm map are also open. See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 25 - Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages|Session 25]].

## Session 26 - current position

- **Ten Sundering objects:** five Coronas of the Ashen Choir and five other relics. The party retains the Bands and their heart stone, without attunement, and suspects that the Staff may have a separate anchor at Tace Nox; this is unproven.
- **Resurrection:** Ballar bought two diamonds (575 gp). One is intended for [[03 People/Notable Figures/Mireya Dinak|Mireya]] and one is held in reserve. The preserved Broken Fang scout is beyond Revivify; neither individual has been revived.
- **Two separate goblin threads:** Malt was released to seek a Pot of Awakening for Bushtache; Ballar has now sent three Bag of Tricks animals towards Tace Nox looking for another goblin who escaped custody around a month earlier.
- **Dazuun lead:** nÃ¢â‚¬â„¢Dong heard testimony from [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Yama and Spopovich|Yama and Spopovich]] about Karessa, Oruc and alleged Rhul-linked match-fixing.
- **Doro:** Ballar heard unverified reports of possible Perfid peace talks involving House Nirrell, with Trenhalde reportedly favourable and Aequinth and Bromere reportedly opposed.
- **Gayle:** [[03 People/Notable Figures/Gayle Bramble|Gayle Bramble]] returned to the Bastion battered and unconscious; Job AshmereÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s whereabouts and the Eden/Summer lead remain unresolved.
- **Next step:** the party intends to consult Romayne and has discussed Temir or Tace Nox, but **has not selected a route**. Hushvale remains deferred.

See [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 26 - Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl|Session 26]].

# Campaign Chronicle

A session-by-session account of the Defracturers' adventures, beginning with the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Emberwake Festival|Emberwake Festival]] and continuing through Rana Viam, the Broken Fang pursuit, the False Gardener, and the partyâ€™s return to Somnolent, encounter with the Sundering memory and new leads from the Dazuun pits.

> [!info] Source coverage
> The supplied document contains individual notes for Sessions 1-11 and 15-26. Sessions 12-14 are preserved as one combined account because the source does not identify reliable boundaries between those sessions. Session 24 is a parallel Bastion interlude covering events in Somnolent during the Defracturers' absence.

**Latest published entry:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 26 - Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl|Session 26: Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl]]

## Session index

- **Session 1:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 01 - Die Dog, Die|Die Dog, Die]] - 22 July 2025. The party meets during Emberwake and defends Somnolent from a coordinated goblin attack.
- **Session 2:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 02 - The Ashes of Emberwake|The Ashes of Emberwake]] - 12 August 2025. The first clues point towards Buggie and a Crow as the party follows Terrick towards Thistlewound.
- **Session 3:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 03 - Into Buggie's Den|Into Buggie's Den]] - 06 September 2025. A tense parley with Buggie reveals new enemies before the party enters the Ardynian ruins beneath Thistlewound.
- **Session 4:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 04 - Tace Nox|Tace Nox]] - 23 September 2025. The party confronts time magic, sacrificial machinery and corrupted relics within Tace Nox.
- **Session 5:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 05 - Fingers, Coins and Incriminations|Fingers, Coins and Incriminations]] - 02 October 2025. The Thistlewound massacre, cursed coins and a roadside ambush deepen the conspiracy around Anthea and Perfid.
- **Session 6:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 06 - Smoke, Song and Secrets|Smoke, Song and Secrets]] - 23 October 2025. Back in Somnolent, each adventurer follows a separate lead involving the Pike, Ardynia and Broken Fang contraband.
- **Session 7:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 07 - Coffee and Contraband|Coffee and Contraband]] - 11 November 2025. Elmwood reveals his interest in Tace Nox, n'Dong uncovers a smuggling network and a cursed mask forces personal reckonings.
- **Session 8:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 08 - The Weight of Secrets|The Weight of Secrets]] - 01 December 2025. Romayne forces the truth into the open as the party pursues Ambassador Prusset through Somnolent.
- **Session 9:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 09 - The Capture of Prusset|The Capture of Prusset]] - 23 December 2025. After a desperate running battle, the party captures Prusset but fractures over who should receive him.
- **Session 10:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 10 - Saints, Spies and Snake Eyes|Saints, Spies and Snake Eyes]] - 04 January 2026. Prusset names the Church behind Anthea's murder while the party infiltrates the One Ball Inn gambling circle.
- **Session 11:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 11 - Ashes, Brands and Windfalls|Ashes, Brands and Windfalls]] - 11 February 2026. A Broken Fang lead, a failed safe robbery and Romayne's formal patronage transform the party into agents of Somnolent.
- **Sessions 12-14:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 12-14 - Fire, Faith and the Corona of the Ashen Choir|Fire, Faith and the Corona of the Ashen Choir]] - 26 March 2026. The Defracturers assault the Perfid embassy, rescue Terrick and pursue the Corona as Prusset is murdered in custody.
- **Session 15:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 15 - Stewards, Saddles and Shambling Roots|Stewards, Saddles and Shambling Roots]] - 27 March 2026. The new Bastion gains staff and horses before the road to Rana Viam begins with a deadly shambling mound.
- **Session 16:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 16 - Cackles, Corpses and Catastrophe|Cackles, Corpses and Catastrophe]] - 08 April 2026. Bandits, Eula, stirges and Cackle Fever turn the journey towards Rana Viam into a disastrous retreat.
- **Session 17:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 17 - Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice|Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice]] - 22 April 2026. After an owlbear attack and an ogre rescue, the party reaches Rana Viam and hears Saint Vaelen's warning.
- **Session 18:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 18 - Frogger, Flames and the Golem's Toll|Frogger, Flames and the Golem's Toll]] - 03 May 2026. The party crosses Rana Viam's living time puzzle and is annihilated by the golem guarding the relic.
- **Session 19:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 19 - Bands, Broken Stone and the Glass Fang|Bands, Broken Stone and the Glass Fang]] - 23 June 2026. The golem falls, the relic is secured and Broken Fang scouts confront the party as they leave Rana Viam.
- **Session 20:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 20 - Plague, Pursuit and Plan E|Plague, Pursuit and Plan E]] - 02 July 2026. The party pursues the scouts, bargains with Plague and captures a map to Ulsar Quinn's camp.
- **Session 21:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 21 - Contracts, Command, and a Burning Camp|Contracts, Command, and a Burning Camp]] - 08 July 2026. Eula claims Salla's plague-ridden body, Ulsar reveals Rhul's Dazuun operation, and the Broken Fang camp burns as Ulsar and Talan fall.
- **Session 22:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 22 - Bodies, Blight, and Bushtache|Bodies, Blight, and Bushtache]]. Eula's purpose becomes clearer, the party discovers what remains of Maelor Vey's corruption and destroys the immediate blight beneath his ruined home.
- **Session 23:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 23 - Ettins, Owlbears, and Betting Losses|Ettins, Owlbears, and Betting Losses]] - 24 August 2026. The party releases Malt with Bushtache, advances Ieuan's Star Seed and continues the return journey toward Somnolent.
- **Session 24:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 24 - Pies, Pike and a one ball blaze|Bastion Interlude: Pies, Pike and the One Ball Blaze]] - 07 September 2026. A parallel Bastion interlude reveals a lead on Eden and Summer, the Pike's departure, Antoinne's disappearance and the destruction of the One Ball Inn.
- **Session 25:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 25 - Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages|Ashes, Arrogance, and the Blood of Ages]] - 12 October 2026. The Defracturers return to Somnolent, confront horrors beneath the One Ball Inn and witness an Ardynian memory revealing the price of the Sundering.
- **Session 26:** [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 26 - Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl|Sundering Relics and the Market Brawl]] - 19 October 2026. The party discusses ten Sundering objects, tangles with Dazuun pit fighters, receives Doroan political rumours and is interrupted by Gayle Brambleâ€™s injured return.

## Major arcs

- **Emberwake and Thistlewound:** Sessions 1-5
- **Secrets in Somnolent:** Sessions 6-11
- **The Perfid embassy:** Sessions 12-14
- **The road to Rana Viam:** Sessions 15-17
- **Rana Viam:** Sessions 18-19
- **The Broken Fang pursuit:** Sessions 20-21
- **The False Gardener:** Session 22
- **Return to Somnolent:** Sessions 23, 25 and 26
- **Bastion downtime:** Session 24, occurring in parallel with the party's absence

## Session 26 reference

New named leads: [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Karessa|Karessa]], [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Oruc|Oruc]] and [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Yama and Spopovich|Yama and Spopovich]]. See [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Open Threads|Open Threads]] for the currently unresolved investigations.

# Session 01: Die Dog, Die

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The party meets during Emberwake and defends Somnolent from a coordinated goblin attack.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 1 |
| **Recorded** | 22 July 2025 at 21:22 |
| **Arc** | Emberwake |

The quiet town of [[Somnolent]] stirred to life with the opening of the [[Emberwake Festival|Emberwake festival]], a celebration of fire, unity, and remembrance. Though joy permeated the air, a sombre undercurrent lingeredâ€”the memory of Anthea Bloodclot, the former leader of the Five Clans, whose passing the year prior cast a long shadow over the festivities.

Three voices opened the day:

- **Romayne Drayton**, the village headman, spoke first. His words were carefully chosenâ€”earnest, charismatic, but subdued, honoring Anthea's memory with the dignity it deserved.
- **Alistair Prusset**, the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] ambassador, followed. He was formal, distant, and faintly dismissive of the festivalâ€™s spiritual elements, though he managed to deliver his remarks with a minimal degree of respect.
- Then came **Callum Redmayne**, landlord of the Drowsy Coin, whose raucous charm and infectious energy kicked off the revelry with cheer and drink.

The party, strangers still, spread across the fairgroundsâ€”each drawn into different distractions, unwittingly threading their fates closer together.

## Festival Highlights

- **Erelmor** stepped boldly into competition, losing a drinking contest to the local legend Billy Hill with no loss of dignity. He later tried his hand at archery with the hunter **Terrick**, who noted Erelmorâ€™s latent potentialâ€”and shared a troubling rumour of a goblin camp spotted near the forest edge.

- **Enrak** found himself the recipient of a warm gesture from the kind-hearted **Xene**, who offered him festival food with a touch of flirtation. Later, he gambled with the mysterious wizard **Elmwood**, but misread the gameâ€”watching the old manâ€™s hands, not the ghostly mage hand that shuffled the cups.

- **Ballar** thundered his way through the games. He bested **Alaaric Hammerfall**, the town treasurer, at diceâ€”perhaps sparking the ember of rivalry. He then crushed the **Hammertoss**, earning the respect of **Balur Moonclaw**, who offered to introduce him to a local fighting pit. Ballar left with two prizes: a **Bag of Rust Tricks** from **Sable**, and a **Hewardâ€™s Handy Haversack**.

- **Ieuan** played for subtler stakes. After pinning the tail perfectly on a donkey (to everyone's surprise), he was rewarded with a **magical key**. He won again at a cup shuffle game, but instead of a prize, chose a private follow-up with Elmwood. Before long, **Mother Joygrove** found him and requested his aid for **Father Zantos** during the coming ritual.

## The Goblin Attack

Just as Father Zantos began the Emberwake invocation, celebration turned to chaos. A dogâ€™s chilling death rattle shattered the ceremony, followed by goblin war-songs. Where the crowd fled, four soon-to-be heroes stood firm.

Erelmor and Ballar surged forward, gleefully trying to outdo one another with cries of â€œwatch this!â€ as they felled goblins in a deadly display of theatre and pride. Meanwhile, Ieuan and Enrak found their fire magic sputtering, failing to ignite fear in the goblinsâ€”but **Ieuanâ€™s Entrapment spell** held the line, ensuring the villagers could flee in safety.

Sensing a greater threat, **Ieuan ignited the bonfire**, intending to warn the townâ€”but instead triggered the next wave. The magical phoenix flare, meant to symbolize rebirth, was in fact a **signal for the goblin warchanter** and her kin to join the fray and burn it all.

Still, the party fought on. Enrak, burning with frustration and flame, stepped into the breach to deliver the **final blow**, incinerating the goblin warchanter and halting the assault. As Father Zantos rushed to tend to the wounded, he declared **Enrak the hero of the hour**â€”a pronouncement that didnâ€™t sit well with his more flamboyant allies.

## Second Wave: Bridge Ambush

No rest for heroesâ€”**a scream rang out** from the bridge. The ambassador, Alistair Prusset, and his loyal hound were under attack. The party sprinted to the scene, but arrived just in time to see the houndâ€™s **throat torn open** by a goblin bossâ€™s dogslicer.

What followed was a mess of half-planned heroics:

- Erelmor finished the goblin dog with efficient violence, before finding the sharp end of a dogslicer and retreating.
- Ballar, ever the opportunist, moved to protect Prusset attempting to slyly curry favour with the ambassador.
- Ieuan and Enrak hurled fire spells againâ€”but the goblin boss seemed warmed at most by the flame, shrugging off their assaults.

A second goblin wave surged from the treeline, surrounding Ballar. From a distance, the others peppered the attackers as best they could.

Eventually, **Erelmor brought down the goblin boss**, and the tide turned. As one last goblin fled and dove into the river to escape, **Ballar drew from his bag of tricks** â€” and a **giant boar** splashed into being, chasing and crushing the fleeing goblin beneath the water.

## Session End

As the blood settled into the earth and the villagers crept cautiously back to assess the wreckage, the four adventurers found themselves **changed**â€”strengthened by danger, tempered by fire, and perhaps for the first time, connected by shared purpose.

They **rose to level 2**, not just as survivors, but as the first sparks of something greater.

# Session 02: The Ashes of Emberwake

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The first clues point towards Buggie and a Crow as the party follows Terrick towards Thistlewound.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 2 |
| **Recorded** | 12 August 2025 at 22:12 |
| **Arc** | Emberwake and Thistlewound |

The firelight had faded, but the embers still glowed. In the aftermath of the goblin attack, *[[Somnolent]]* stirred with caution. The party, newly forged in battle, now stood beneath smoke-choked skies unsure of what came next.

**Ambassador Prusset**, still reeling from the death of his faithful hound, met the group in grim silence. **Ballar** stepped forward with a gesture of respect, offering to see to the dogâ€™s burial personally. Prusset, ever the diplomat, declinedâ€”keeping his grief neatly folded behind icy eyes.

Before tensions could rise further, **Romayne Drayton** arrived to reclaim control. Measured and authoritative, the village headman called for calm, asked the party to visit him in the morning, and promised to speak of next steps.

The group searched the goblin dead and found two strange clues:

- A **note** scrawled in barely-legible goblin:
  *"No kill, just scare. Get Pruzet. Crow want alive. Buggie."*
- A **large silver coin**, odd in make and unmarked by any known mint.

Prusset denied knowledge of the name "Buggie," and Drayton, while unfamiliar with the goblin, noted with unease that "Crow" had once referred to **Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s hidden agents**, the quiet, loyal few who operated in her shadow.

The heroes took their leave, agreeing to regroup at the Drowsy Coin in the morning. Each went their own wayâ€”with Ballar returning to the **[[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] consulate**. There, he encountered **Ambassador Ouin**, a woman of silk and sharp instincts, caught off guard by the muddy menagerie that trailed behind Ballar. Upon hearing of Ballarâ€™s involvement in the ambassadorâ€™s rescue, Ouin gave an approvingâ€”if wearyâ€”nod, but warned: *â€œRemember where your loyalties lie. Doro first. Always.â€*

## Picking up Threads and Receipts

Morning broke with grease and gossip. Over **breakfast boar baps** at the Drowsy Coin, the group reunited and learned that **Elmwood**, the local wizard, had performed powerful magic to protect the tavern and its patrons during the attackâ€”a fact shared with no small amount of admiration by **Callum Redmayne**, which seemed to inspire some jealousy in **Enrak**.

They then returned to **Romayne Drayton**, who spoke openly and plainly. With the village still rattled and official aid slow to arrive, he asked for the partyâ€™s support in uncovering the truth behind the attack. To assist, he offered one of his trusted agentsâ€”**nâ€™Dong**.

Drayton laid bare the fragile unity left in Antheaâ€™s wake. She had been more than a leaderâ€”she was the very thread holding the Five Clans together. Her â€œCrowsâ€ were real, though anonymous. Now, only the **Voruun** and **Khaali** still marched toward her dream. **Erzhin** had withdrawn, and **Orvak**, plagued by its **[[Rootbound]]** movement, teetered on the edge of internal collapse. Surprisingly, he also admitted that Bloodclot had very few loyal to her in Somnolent, a town that prided itself on its neutrality and ability to stay out of clan skirmishes, but if anyone was still loyal to Anthea then **Eden**, the Voruun liaison, would know.

With no clear trail to follow but the goblin note, Drayton suggested seeking out the hunter **Terrick** or reviewing the **report** he had submitted.

Thus, the party sought out **Alaaric Hammerfall**, treasurer of Somnolent and a man not easily parted from his ledgers. After some bartering, Alaaric sold them **Terrickâ€™s report** for a single gold coin. Ballar, ever the dealmaker, pressed furtherâ€”inquiring about a report on **Antheaâ€™s death**. Alaaric confirmed its existence, but explained the delicate material would take some time to procure.

## Chasing Waterfalls

With the trail confirmed, the party visited Terrick himself in the **village arcade**. He was eager to helpâ€”though **Erelmor**, in an almost performative show of suspicion, accused the man of failing to hunt his own rabbits. Only **Ieuan** and **nâ€™Dongâ€™s** timely intervention calmed the rising tension. Terrick offered to guide the group to **Thistlewound**, the site of his goblin sighting, but warned they should arrive before nightfall.

Their journey was swift but not uneventful. Hours into the trip, they arrived at a river, where a **waterfall roared across their path**. Terrick, showing surprising agility, demonstrated a hidden **rope swing** and leapt across with ease.

**Ieuan**, inspired by the display, attempted the sameâ€”and promptly botched it. With a *sickening splash*, he slammed into the river and immediately drew the attention of a **crocodile**. As the beast snapped its jaws around him, **Ballar dove in** to assist, slashing at thin air as the rest of the party fired spells and arrows.

With death looming, **Ieuan** thrust his hands into the beastâ€™s mouth and unleashed a toxic blast of **poison spray** down its throat, felling it from within. Terrick pulled him to safety as the other crocs were dispatched.

Amid the aftermath, **Ieuan spotted something behind the waterfall**. Working with **Enrak**, the two used their **mage hands** to retrieve a waterlogged **wooden cache**, which contained **eight healing potions**â€”a windfall they would soon be grateful for.

## Obvious Riddles & Unexpected Bullywugs

The final leg of their journey led them to the crumbling edges of **Thistlewound Ruins**, overgrown and silent.

**Ieuan**, attuned to whispers on the wind, caught goblin voices murmuring in the distance. Soon after, the group encountered a **sealed stone door** bearing an inscription:

*The moon and stars may show the way, To knowledge lost, kept sealed away.*

*I guard what sleeps where silence grows, No flame may flicker, no lantern glow.*

*In still of dark, speak sleep's last shout, Snuff the candles and call lights out.*

Ieuan instantly divined the meaning: *Return at night, in darkness, and speak â€œLights Out.â€* It was, in his own words, *â€œobvious.â€*

With time to kill, the party scouted furtherâ€”**Erelmor**, perhaps too eager, **triggered a trap**, alerting a mix of **goblin and bullywug guards**. The ensuing battle was dramatic, chaotic, and deeply pyrotechnic.

- Spells flew with reckless abandon.
- **Erelmor** doused a bullywug in oil just to see it burn.
- **nâ€™Dong** and **Enrak** emptied their arcane reserves in wild displays of poison and flame.

More enemies surged from the shadowsâ€”but just before bloodshed could resume, a massive **bugbear** emerged from the cave entrance, barking a command:

> **â€œEnough! If you want answers, we talk. Not fight.â€**

The battlefield fell still.

# Session 03: Into Buggie's Den

> [!abstract] Session summary
> A tense parley with Buggie reveals new enemies before the party enters the Ardynian ruins beneath Thistlewound.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 3 |
| **Recorded** | 06 September 2025 at 14:19 |
| **Arc** | Thistlewound |

## Into the Bugbearâ€™s Den

In the damp throat of the wilds, beneath the looming stone of Thistlewound and the weight of unseen eyes, the party stood face-to-face with a monster.

**Buggie**, towering and scarred, was not the frothing warbeast they expected, but a warlord weary of blood. Behind him, goblins and bugbears watched in silenceâ€”not the frenzied swarm that had assaulted [[Somnolent]], but sullen, displaced kin, licking old wounds beneath mossy banners.

The tension was immediate and knife-sharp. The party, never ones to flinch, **openly admitted to killing Ryzdak**, Buggieâ€™s commander, and **Erelmor** in particular seemed to savour the admission. Buggie, to his credit, did not rise to anger. Instead, he spoke of what truly mattered: **a coin** Ryzdak carriedâ€”an insurance policy.

Feigning ignorance, the party offered a deal. In return for information on the **Crow** who had hired them, they would help recover the coin. But patience was wearing thin. Words stretched taut. Finally, an accord was struck.

**Buggie would give them everything he had**: names, fragments, and a **document** smuggled at the Crowâ€™s requestâ€”her last order before she disappeared. The failure to capture Ambassador Prusset meant, Buggie admitted with cold certainty, *they would likely never see her again.*

With all eyes watching, **Erelmor clumsily fumbled the handoff**, forcing **Ballar** to complete the transaction. As his fingers closed around the object, he realized the truth: this was no ordinary trinket. It was a **ceremonial coin**, minted in **[[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]]**, bearing the unmistakable crest of **House Bromere**.

Before they could leave, a **goblin warchanter demanded retribution** for her sisterâ€™s death. **Erelmor**, quick-witted even under threat, wove a half-truthâ€”claiming they saw her die, yes, but not by their hand. The lie held. Barely.

As they turned to leave, **nâ€™Dong**, silent till now, **pressed Buggie about a name from his pastâ€”Prodosius**. The bugbear's answer was chilling. His tribe had **fled [[The Powers That Be#Minir|Minir]]** to escape the Broken Fang, a ruthless force growing bolder, expanding from their borderland camp near **[[Clan Erzhin]]**. Of Prodosius, Buggie knew littleâ€”but enough to stoke a fire in nâ€™Dongâ€™s heart.

## Putting the pieces together

Smoke still clung to their clothes as the party emerged from the goblin hideout, the echoes of parlay with **Buggie the bugbear warlord** lingering in the air. The uneasy truce held, but beneath the surfaceâ€”suspicion festered.

As the group trudged through the underbrush, **Ieuanâ€™s jaw tightened**, eyes fixed on **nâ€™Dong**. Without warning, he pulled the towering dragonborn aside, voice low and sharp.

**nâ€™Dong didnâ€™t flinch.** Instead, he exhaled a truth that hung heavy in the forest air. He had once been **enslaved by Prodosius**, a bugbear warlord whose name still made his fists curl. He hadnâ€™t joined this quest for coin or glory. Heâ€™d seen the word *bugbear* in that crude goblin note and followed it like a bloodhound, knowing vengeance might lie at the end of the trail. Trust, once strained, began to realignâ€”though not fully heal.

With spell slots empty, the party finally took some time to breathe â€” and think. They turned over what they had learnt like bones in a fire, watching the shadows shift and dance, and piece by bitter piece, a story began to form.

Theories became conviction as **Ieuan**, **Enrak**, **Ballar**, **Erelmor**, and **nâ€™Dong** laid the web bare:

- Anthea Bloodclot didnâ€™t die by fate. She was silenced.
- The [[Perfidian Faith|Church of Elgira]], threatened by her influence and unity, whispered the command.
- And it was Prusset, the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] ambassador, who gave the orderâ€”using a shadowy Orvaki faction called the [[Rootbound]] to do the deed.

The assassination of Anthea was not just a murderâ€”it was a **betrayal of the Five Clans**, a scalpel wound to their fragile alliance. Now, at least one of her hidden **Crows** was calling in old debts, with **Prusset** in her sights.

## Into the dark

Before them loomed the sealed stone threshold of **Thistlewound**, the ruins whispering secrets older than the clans themselves. *"Call Lights Out"* The words had lingered since Ieuan had first deciphered the ancient riddle, and now, as twilight clung to the edges of the forest, the moment had come.

There was hesitation. Some advised caution. Others waited for a sign.

**Erelmor** did not.

With the recklessness of a man chasing ghosts, he stepped into the dark without waiting for his allies. The others followed, some with blades drawn, some with prayers muttered. **Ballar**, bringing up the rear, exhaled a long-suffering sigh that echoed faintly into the depths.

The air shifted the moment they crossed the threshold. **Suffocating pressure**, oppressive and humid, settled over them like a damp shroud. The ruins pulsed with a sickly **green light**, and something unnatural stirred in the walls.

They crept through narrow halls when a **Chwinga**â€”a flickering, fey-like spiritâ€”darted across their path and vanished through solid stone. A harmless omen, perhaps. But to nerves already taut, it felt like a warning.

In the first chamber, a flash of light drew their eyes to a grim statue of an **[[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] soldier**. A staff vanished from its grasp, swallowed by unseen forces. Instinct screamed at them to turn back. Instead, they doubled down.

**Ballar**, growing suspicious, cast forth a **brown bear** from his Bag of Rust Tricks, using the creature as a trap tester.

Meanwhile, **Ieuan** shifted into the form of a rat to scout ahead. Neither beast found any trapsâ€”but what they did find was worse: **bodies**. Dozens. Strewn across rooms and corridors, decaying in robes of faded glory. The stink of **death** clung to the stone like moss.

And yet, amidst this desecrated sanctum, **Erelmor** looked up at the jeweled eyes of the statue and thought, *I want those.* He attempted to scale itâ€”failedâ€”and fell with a graceless thud. Rat-Ieuan bit him on the neck in reprimand.

Their path led them to what remained of a **living quarter**, aged and dust-choked but intact. Among cracked tomes and shattered furniture, they uncovered:

- An **Orb of Time**
- Books cataloguing **creatures of immense lifespans**â€”liches, dragons, phoenixes.
- And most damning of all, a **discarded letter** penned by **Anthea Bloodclot** herself, revealing she had once entered these ruins and encountered strange puzzles and deadlier things still.

Anthea's letter confirmed the origin of the ruins. **Ardynian.** The name alone was enough to chill **nâ€™Dong**, who confessed in a rare moment of openness: *It was ruins like theseâ€¦ Ardynianâ€¦ that twisted me into what I am.* The others could only wonder what had once happened to him in the dark.

In a tomb-like chamber, the bearâ€”still loyally blundering aheadâ€”**kicked open a door**, revealing three monstrous **giant centipedes**. Acting quickly, Erelmor turned the ground into chaos, flinging a sack of **ball bearings** to slow their advance.

It didnâ€™t work.

The pedes simply climbed the walls and descended upon the party from above.

The fight was fast and vicious, but the party stood strong. **Ballar**, ever the finisher, struck the final blowâ€”again.

While Erelmor grudgingly gathered up his scattered bearings, the others investigated:

- **Ballar and Enrak** retrieved more **healing potions** from near the sarcophagi.
- **nâ€™Dong** found three **jade crystals** embedded in an altar, which he pocketed swiftlyâ€”drawing a glare from Erelmor.
- A **mosaic** confirmed the earlier noteâ€™s clue: the solution to the ruinâ€™s puzzle lay in creating an **arrow of light** pointing toward the altar.

**Ballar**, seized by a sudden burst of industrious madness, ran through the ruins **tearing sconces from the walls**, hoping to piece together the arrow manually.

While Ballar made his frantic rounds, **nâ€™Dong, Ieuan, and Enrak** discovered the real puzzle: an ancient mechanism already half-triggered by the bearâ€™s heavy paws.

Panic set in. Had they already failed?

They regrouped, tensions high. Ballar scolded his bear. The others argued. Then, without warning, **Erelmor and nâ€™Dong** stepped forward â€” and triggered the puzzle prematurely.

Green light **flickered** violently through the chamber. The ruins screamed. From the shadows emerged a **skeletal warhorse**, its hooves crackling with unnatural energy. The battle was fierce, but fleeting. The party, galvanized by fear, fell upon it. **Ballar**, resolute and righteous, delivered the final strikeâ€”again.

Not wanting to tempt fate a second time, they approached the puzzle with care. **Ieuan** scratched diagrams into the dust, frantically testing combinations. **Ballar** declared it impossible. **Erelmor**, tired of the noise, **found the answer**.

With a shudder and a groan, the puzzle accepted their resolution. The green light pulsed, focused, and coalesced into a swirling **portal above the altar**.

It opened. But what lay beyond?

# Session 04: Tace Nox

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The party confronts time magic, sacrificial machinery and corrupted relics within Tace Nox.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 4 |
| **Recorded** | 23 September 2025 at 21:20 |
| **Arc** | Tace Nox |

## Relics and Ruin

The puzzle was solved, the path was open â€” but beyond dark flickering with green light, only deeper shadow waited. A narrow tunnel beckoned, too much of a squeeze for Snippy the bear. As the loyal creature let out a low, uncertain huff, Ballar gave him a gentle pat and a quiet word: â€œWait here. Weâ€™ll be back.â€

They would not.

The next chamber felt colderâ€”not in temperature, but in spirit. At its center rose a column of flickering emerald light, strange and pulsing like a heartbeat. Suspended within were two butterflies, motionless in stasis. To either side, a Chwinga lounged upon stone ottomans, unmoving, unblinking, uncaring.

The party approached gently this timeâ€”offering food, soft words, and hesitant hands. The chwinga remained indifferent. At least they werenâ€™t hostile.

The ottomans, however, held more interest. nâ€™Dong stepped up to open oneâ€¦ and failed spectacularly, slipping backward with a thud as if repelled by the stone itself. Ballar, with a grunt and a flex, opened it with easeâ€”finding 100 gold and a Ring of Mind Shielding, which Ballar quietly equipped and hid without mention.

The other ottoman held more goldâ€”shared more freelyâ€”and a hagâ€™s eye, which vanished swiftly into Erelmorâ€™s possession.

They turned at last to the green column. An inscription lay etched at its base:

â€œSlowed by radiance and trapped in time,

Release the static from their binds.

One to protect you from the relics,

One to illuminate the exit.â€

They tried everything: brute force, magic, cleverness. But it was nâ€™Dong and Ballar, acting in tandem, who reached into the stream of power and retrieved their rewards:

- A mysterious magical staff, humming with latent time magic.
- A gem of brightness, blazing with inner light.

The moment they were freed, the portcullis ahead slid open, beckoning them onward.

## The crucible of stolen time

They descended into a narrow corridor, each step heavy with tension, until it opened into a long-abandoned laboratory, its very air vibrating with arcane pulses.

There, they found three caged hags, suspended in twisted magical stasis, each one a flickering shell of its former self. In the centre: a dais and plinth, the perfect size for the Orb of Time.

Erelmor, ever impulsive, plunged his hand into one of the magical cages â€” nearly losing it in the process. Ballar threw himself at a sealed door, to no avail. But Enrak, combing the desk, found:

- A rune-sealed scroll bearing the sigil of a sand timer.
- A note, left by an unknown [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] researcher, explaining the ritualâ€™s purpose: Life, traded for progress.

So, with no other option presenting itself, they began. Erelmor, first to act, placed the orb and initiated the siphoning ritual. As arcane light wrapped around him, one hag crumbled to ashâ€”and he felt restored, empowered, like after a long rest. A giftâ€¦ bought with a life. nâ€™Dong followed, then Ieuan, who watched as the second hag crumbled to dust. The sealed door opened, allowing further passageâ€”but the final hag remained, twitching in half-life.

They hesitated. Morality battled pragmatism. Then Ballar, in an act of grim compromise, tossed a goat from his Bag of Rust Tricks onto the dais. The goat bleated once, then froze in mid-scream as the ritual drained it. The third hag died. The goat turned to stone.

Before they could reflect, a chilling sound cut through the ruins. Snippyâ€”their stalwart bearâ€”was dying.

## The guardian and the relics

Racing through the newly opened path, the party arrived at a grand stone chamber where the Guardian awaitedâ€”its massive form carved in Ardynian fashion, animated, and now barring their exit.

In silence, it extended a hand, beckoning the party to return the staff. nâ€™Dong refused. Erelmor fired.

The guardian reeled back â€” but not in retreat. It stepped aside, revealing the true threat: relics of hags and dwarves, corrupted by the same magic that had defiled these ruins, now rising to attack. Ieuan, with sudden clarity, darted forward to yank the staff from nâ€™Dongâ€™s grasp and place it in the guardianâ€™s hand.

The construct nodded â€” and responded with power. A protective aura washed over the party, granting bonus vitality and resistance for the battle ahead. Then the room erupted into war.

## The time war

The Relics were monsters of another age â€” screeching, relentless, forged from bone and corruption. The party met them head-on.

- Ieuan, now a hulking elk, gored and slammed his way through the battlefield.
- Ballar, as ever, stood unflinching in the storm, holding the line against four dwarven relics â€” all weakened by Snippyâ€™s final stand.
- Erelmor and Enrak flung spells like fire and daggers, dancing between foes.
- nâ€™Dong, cold and calculating, let loose his arcane fury.

The guardian fought beside themâ€¦ but was the first to fall. Its stone frame cracked and shattered under the hagsâ€™ savage assault, the time-magic staff falling to the floor. The tide turned.

Ieuan, desperate to shift momentum, cast Faerie Fire, lighting the enemies like candles â€” despite catching Ballar in the blaze. It wasnâ€™t enough. Ballar went down, blood soaking the ruin floor. Ieuan followed, cut down by a hagâ€™s screeching magic.

Now just three remained. Outnumbered, outmatched, and nearly out of spells, Enrak, Erelmor, and nâ€™Dong made their final stand. There was no glory â€” only grit. They fought with the cold knowledge that failure here meant dying forgotten in an ancient tomb.

And then, through the smoke and blood, nâ€™Dong raised his hand. A Magic Missile, three bolts of pure force. The chamber fell silent.

## The Price of Power

The remaining few still standing dragged their allies back from the brink with potions and trembling hands. Together, they searched the rubble â€” reclaiming the Staff of Broken Time, and prying free emeralds from the guardianâ€™s shattered face.

Victory. At last.

But as they stepped across the threshold to leaveâ€¦ everything changed.

The staff vanished. The gems disintegrated. The Ardynian ruin, ever greedy, took back its gifts. What it left themâ€¦ was horror.

The quiet clearing of Thistlewound was gone. In its place, a slaughterfield. Blood soaked the earth, limbs and steel lay scattered, the grass torn to shreds.

And among the carnage, the party stood blinkingâ€”alive, but only just. And the war, it seemed, had already begun.

# Session 05: Fingers, Coins and Incriminations

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The Thistlewound massacre, cursed coins and a roadside ambush deepen the conspiracy around Anthea and Perfid.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 5 |
| **Recorded** | 02 October 2025 at 11:14 |
| **Arc** | Return from Thistlewound |

## Thrones, Fires and Fingers

Smoke still clung to their lungs and blood to their boots as the party stepped out of the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] ruin, hoping for daylight, perhaps clarity. Instead, they found **carnage**.

What had once been the **clearing of Thistlewound** - quiet, still, strangely untouched â€” was now a stage set for horror. Bodies lay in ruin, **twisted and torn**, blood slicking the grass, steel shattered, the stink of death rising thick and wet from the soil.

The party spread out, hearts racing, stomachs tight.

- **Ieuan**, ever perceptive, knelt by a corpse that didnâ€™t belongâ€”a **drow**, out of place, his tunic torn and stained in dark violet. No one knew who he was. But someone had wanted him dead.
- **Enrak** examined another, noting the **brutality of it all**â€”both **blades and claws** had done the work. This wasnâ€™t clean. It wasnâ€™t professional. It was **desperate**.
- **Ballar** found what remained of **Buggie**, the bugbear warlord whoâ€™d once ruled this sorry patch of forest. He tore through the corpse, searchingâ€”urgently, almost obsessivelyâ€”for the **strange silver coin** he'd handed over just hours earlier. He found **nothing**.

Unseen by the others, **Erelmor** slipped away. Perhaps out curiosity or his rogue instinct to act alone. He crept toward the edge of the massacre, eyes peeled, bow nocked. A branch snapped. He spun, arrow readyâ€”but held his fire just in time as **Terrick** emerged from the trees, eyes wide, a single **terrified goblin** trailing behind him like a lost child.

The party reconvened, pressing both survivors for answers, but got little more than fragments. The attackers â€” whoever they were â€” had come **looking for something**. A **note**, they said. Stolen by Buggieâ€™s gang.

That was enough for **Ballar**, who wasted no time declaring the goblin their **â€œguest.â€** His tone didnâ€™t leave room for refusal.

**Terrick**, understandably, protested the idea of returning to [[Somnolent]] without rest â€” but was overruled. There was **something hunting in these woods**, and the party didnâ€™t intend to sleep where it might find them. As **nâ€™Dong, Enrak, and Ieuan** decided to search Buggieâ€™s Den, **Ballar** made a quiet request of **Erelmor**: a trophy. Something to show **Alistair Prusset**. Something to prove Buggie was truly dead. A **finger**, perhaps.

Erelmor, ever the dramatist, had a grander visionâ€”**the head**. Unfortunately, enthusiasm far outpaced skill. He hacked away, **missing the neck entirely**, tearing through **muscle and bone**, coating himself in bugbear blood like a mad butcher. When it was finally too much even for him, he took the finger. Just the finger. A **sickening rip**, and it was done. But something else dropped from the grasp of the dead bugbear chief; an insignia ripped from one of the assassins â€” one Erelmor immediately recognised as belonging to his old allies **the rootbound**.

Meanwhile the rest of the party inspected the cave that had once echoed with goblin songs and bullywug croaks. There, **nâ€™Dong** uncovered a **hidden compartment** beneath the stone throne. Inside: **50 gold**, and more importantly, **the strange silver coin**. The one Buggie had demanded be returned to him.

Then, for reasons only the gods might understand, **nâ€™Dong, Enrak, and Ieuan** decided to **torch the camp**. Whether it was vengeance, cleansing, or pure spite was unclearâ€”but the smoke rose high into the night as they **stumbled coughing** from the cave, eyes stinging, lungs scorched.

They emerged to see Buggie's corpse defiled, a shocking scene of Erelmor's "handiwork". nâ€™Dong vomited on the grass.

## Blood for gold

With all â€œloose endsâ€ tied, corpses looted and burned, trophies in hand, and a shell-shocked goblin in tow, the group turned toward the **moonlit path back to Somnolent**. Still bloodied from the ruins, lungs raw from smoke, and nerves stretched thin, the party trudged deeper into the night. Their path home was far from peacefulâ€”and fate had no intention of granting them rest.

They found them by moonlight: a **ragtag band of outlaws**, deep in debate. Sat in a loose circle, they bickered over a **haul of gold**â€”not how to spend it, but whether to **keep it** at all. Voices cracked with unease. Fear and greed clouded their thoughts, hanging over their fire like smoke. It should have been a warning.

But **Ballar**, bold as brass and tired of walking, stepped into their midst and invoked the image of Talik the laughing coin as he sang: *â€œSpinner of fortunes.â€.* It didnâ€™t go down well. Though the bandits outnumbered them and the party was battered, bruised, and sleepless, **the Squishy Boys did not falter**. As blades were drawn and magic flared, battle erupted in the forest clearing.

As the clash began, their **goblin prisoner** seized his chance. With Terrick fumbling behind, the goblin darted into the treesâ€”gone like a whisper. **Ballar**, still dripping with the blood of the bandit leader he'd just crushed, rounded on Terrick with a **burning fury**, all but roaring his incompetence before launching into the woods in pursuit.

Meanwhile, **nâ€™Dongâ€™s magic missile** carved through the remaining bandits like divine judgment, ending the skirmish in a flash of arcane light. Back at the treeline, **Ieuan**, flanked by a sheepish Terrick and a furious Ballar, somehow managed to persuade the goblin to stay put, despite Ballar making no attempt to hide that the goblin would die in Somnolent.

But something **stranger** was happening behind the battleline. As **Erelmor** plucked a **scimitar** from the fallen bandit leader and immediately tossed it away with an audible sneer (â€œworthlessâ€), a **hooded woman** appeared from **thin air**â€”no footfall, no warning, only presence.

Without urgency or fear, she began to **collect the bodies** of the fallen with a unerring professionalism. She looked to Erelmor, calm and businesslike: â€œSixty gold, for the effort.â€

He snatched the gold like a dragon claiming tribute. â€œSee you again soon,â€ she said, with a knowing smirk. Then she was **gone**, vanishing into nothing as if the trees had swallowed her whole.

**nâ€™Dong and Enrak stood frozen**, mouths open, the hairs on their arms prickling. It was **not right**. Something **deeply wrong** had just happened. When the group reconvened, **Ieuan listened grimly** as nâ€™Dong and Enrak relayed what they'd seen. Whatever **magic** or **power** had just traded with Erelmorâ€”it wasnâ€™t natural. It wasnâ€™t safe.

They turned their attention to the **banditâ€™s gold**, hoping to salvage something usefulâ€”only to find it **tainted**, laced with **arcane markers**. Tracking magic. Cursed money. Erelmor, still holding his bounty, curled his fingers tighter. They warned him. Pleaded. He *accused nâ€™Dong* of hoarding goldâ€”**the irony lost entirely** on him.

Eventually peer pressure swayed the decision, Erelmor threw 50 gold into the bandits pile. **Ieuan**, ever patient, watched him like a schoolmaster catching a student cheat. He didnâ€™t need wildshape to see the greed. He simply folded his arms and waited.

Eventually, **begrudgingly**, **Erelmor gave up the last of the cursed coin**, flinging it away with the petulance of a child forced to return stolen sweets.

## Fighting against the current

With the cursed coins discarded and the woods behind them still humming with uneasy silence, the party resumed their journey back to Somnolent. The strange hooded womanâ€”who had so casually collected corpses and vanished into mistâ€”remained a dark question on everyoneâ€™s mind. There were no answers to be found in the trees, only more shadows. But the path ahead, unfortunately, held familiar dangers.

As they neared the river crossing once more, the haunting sound of rushing water stirred an involuntary shudder in Ieuan, his last encounter with the waterfall still fresh in memory. Ballar, less sentimental and more impatient, bristled at the delay and announced his intention to go firstâ€”commanding the goblin prisoner to follow once he was across. With the force and flair of a seasoned athlete, Ballar gripped the rope swing and sailed through the air, landing with practiced ease on the far bank. What he didnâ€™t expect was the welcoming committee.

From the underbrush emerged two robed figuresâ€”**priests of Elgira**. Ballar had landed in their trap, one cast *Zone of Truth*, the other *Hold Person*. Ballar resisted the first with sheer force of will, but the paralysis took hold. Locked in place, unable to speak or move, he listened as they coldly demanded to know the whereabouts of the **stolen letter from Prussetâ€™s study**. Ballar held his nerve, managing to obfuscate that the note was in his bag. The priests gave their judgment in the form of crueltyâ€”**kicking Ballar into the river** like a sack of meat. He crashed down the waterfall, armor and limbs colliding with rock and current, vanishing from view.

From the far bank, the party watched the scene unfold and erupted into action. Firebolts, acid and conjured daggers flew across the river in a hailstorm of retaliation. The battle quickly turned savage. **Radiant flames** licked through the trees as the priests retaliated, catching **Erelmor off guard** and dropping him to the ground, unconscious. In the chaos, the goblin prisonerâ€”finally sensing true freedomâ€”made its escape, darting into the undergrowth and out of their grasp for good.

Dragging himself from the river with water clinging to his skin and fury in his veins, **Ballar broke the paralysis through sheer rage**, staggered upright, and locked eyes with the cliff above. With a roar, he **charged up the 30-foot incline**, scaling it with maddened determination and unleashing his gem of brightness in a wave of light that lit the enemy up like a beacon. The rest of the gang followed his lead, their anger boiling over. The last of Elgiraâ€™s enforcers fell shortly afterâ€”**crushed beneath steel and spell**.

As the dust settled, **Ieuan rushed to Erelmorâ€™s side**, stabilizing him with practiced hands and a rare moment of tenderness. The party, battered and breathless, took a moment to breathe. Across the river, the rope swing still hung like a dare.

With Ballar alone on the far bank, Ieuan took it upon himself to rally the team, offering **Guidance and encouragement** in equal measure as each member made their crossing. The moment of calm was almost pleasantâ€”until **Terrick**, who had contributed little during the battle beyond missed shots and nervous mumbling, **somersaulted elegantly across the ravine** with a flourish. The entire party stared, somewhere between disbelief and disgust. It took all of Ieuanâ€™s inner strength, but he followed nextâ€”**conquering his fears** and landing safely at last.

Bruised, soaked, exhausted, and still haunted by too many unanswered questions, the group finally saw the flickering lanterns of **Somnolent** on the horizon. The town had not yet stirredâ€”still cradled in the final hours of night. Wordlessly, they agreed to rest. The sun would rise soon, and with it, **new threats, new truths, and perhaps darker tides still to come**.

# Session 06: Smoke, Song and Secrets

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Back in Somnolent, each adventurer follows a separate lead involving the Pike, Ardynia and Broken Fang contraband.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 6 |
| **Recorded** | 23 October 2025 at 20:15 |
| **Arc** | Somnolent investigations |

## 4AM â€“ The Walk of Shame

The Squishy Boys stumbled back into [[Somnolent]] under the cloak of predawn silence â€” bloodied, burned, and barely upright. Their return did not go unnoticed. Deputy Gayle Bramble was waiting. With arms crossed and eyes sharp, she dressed down Terrick for his impromptu adventure, making it clear that his wife, Rowena, had been worried sick. The ranger sheepishly scurried home to whatever frosty reception awaited him.

Gayle passed on one final message: Romayne Drayton expected a full report that evening.

The group scattered, agreeing on a 1PM rendezvous at the Drowsy Coin. Ballar suggested rest.

Ballar, of course, ignored his own advice.

## 4:30AM â€“ Ballar Gets a Step Ahead

Taking a moment to make sure he was perfumed to perfection, Ballar made his way to the [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] Embassy. Inside the cold marble halls, he was met by Stacii, Ambassador Ouin Brond's ever-prickly assistant. After some theatrical insistence, she begrudgingly let him through.

Ouin was pensive, glass of wine in hand, uninterested at first â€” until Ballar began talking. He revealed everything: the Bromere coin, the [[Rootbound]], Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s assassination, and more. Ouin dismissed any direct Bromere involvement in the abduction attempt, but pointed Ballar toward a potential link â€” Ervain â€œThe Pike,â€ a former Bromere commander frequenting a dice game at the One Ball Inn, run by Alaaric Hammerfall.

Ouin encouraged caution, asking Ballar to keep his findings quiet until they could be exploited. She reminded him that secrets were a currency of his family and challenged him to keep his unpredictable companions in check. Ballar noddedâ€¦ somewhat evasively.

Before leaving, Ballar leveraged Ouin's goodwill to press for more on the Broken Fang, the mercenary group nâ€™Dong had mentioned. She was only too eager to point them out on the map, confirming they had disrupted Doro trade routes and promising to provide more intelligence.

Mission complete, Ballar headed to the gym to get a pump on, followed by a well-earned trance in the sauna.

## 8AM â€“ Stars, Smoke and Ashes

Elsewhere, Erelmor tranced in the quiet solitude of Dustmere Grounds, but found no peace. A song crept into his thoughts, unbidden and sinister â€” a melody from the Maw, tied to the darkest day of his life. His trance spiralled into a vivid memory: smoke, ash, his home in ruin.

The song was real.

He awoke to hear it drifting from a drunken trader. Without hesitation, Erelmor tackled the man into the bushes, blade to throat. Desperate, panicked, the man revealed he was a former House Daskel soldier. Heâ€™d heard the tune earlier that morningâ€”someone had sung it at a dice game in the One Ball Inn to taunt a man named Ervain.

Almost sated, Erelmor rifled through the manâ€™s belongings, claiming a handful of coins and a dram of whiskey, warning him never to sing of The Pike again.

Consumed by rage, Erelmor charged to the One Ball Inn. Finding it closed, he fought the urge to break in, instead wandering toward the arcade, simmering, waiting.

Meanwhile, Ieuan awoke in quiet contemplation, only to find Father Zantos waiting. The two spoke at length. Zantos praised Ieuanâ€™s choice to walk the Circle of Stars, linking it to Orun the Verdant Kin. Their conversation turned to the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynians]], with Zantos expressing worry at how recklessly the party had disturbed the ruins. He shared his upbringing in [[Clan Erzhin]], where ancient truths about the Ardynians were kept in the Annals of Stoneâ€”knowledge forgotten elsewhere in [[Rakara]].

Talk shifted to the Rootbound. Though both men were guarded, Ieuan learned that Zantos once called himself a member, before withdrawing when the group turned too rebellious, even murderous. Zantos was heartbroken to hear they might now be aligned with Elgiraâ€™s Church and [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]].

As they parted, Zantos offered a gift: a Star Seed, promising it would bloom once Ieuan fully embraced his druidic path.

## 10AM â€“ Making a Mark

Refreshed, dressed in finery and fully attuned to his Ring of Mind Shielding, Ballar tried to claim reimbursement from Stacii, but was met with the usual Doro bureaucracy. Undeterred, he marched to the Silent Court and was finally shown to Alaaricâ€™s office, where the treasurer unlocked a complicated safe and handed over the Anthea Bloodclot death report.

Not done yet, Ballar leaned into his next objectiveâ€”infiltrating the dice game. Playing the role of curious newcomer, he managed to catch Alaaricâ€™s interest. The price of admission: 250 gold, plus 25 gold per companion watching. Ballar, ever the opportunist, now had one foot firmly inside Somnolentâ€™s underbelly.

## 12PM â€“ Alcohol-Fueled Escapades

nâ€™Dong woke in the woods, sun dappling through the trees. After a failed attempt at river fishing, he heard a racket from upriver. A man floated by on a makeshift barrel-raft, clearly up to no good.

nâ€™Dong sprinted ahead, reaching the bridge just as the raft passed beneath. Without hesitation, he leaptâ€”crashing onto the raft with a thud that nearly capsized it. A quick Suggestion spell convinced the smuggler to dock the raft and turn over the goods, but as they reached shore, nâ€™Dong slipped, face-planting in the shallows.

The man tried to flee, only to be snagged by nâ€™Dongâ€™s clawed grip. During the interrogation, the man admitted he was just the delivery boy. Heâ€™d make the dropâ€¦ and someone else would take the loot. That was all nâ€™Dong needed.

Volunteering himself as cargo, nâ€™Dong climbed into one of the whiskey barrels. The journey was long, wet, and deeply uncomfortableâ€”but he stayed the course.

Meanwhile, Enrak woke in a Drowsy Coin filled with drunken song. At the centre was Ersil Loquella, bard and rascal, leading a rousing tavern anthem about Enrak the Brazen Flame. Delighted, Enrak cast Firebolt into the hearth â€” accidentally scorching the bricks â€” and bought another round.

Between drinks, Ersil pressed for stories. Enrak spoke warmly of his companions: Ballar the Bold, nâ€™Dong the Poisonous, Erelmor the Gold-Seeker, and Ieuan the Mystical. Though he rejected the notion of being the group's leader, he did share his ambition â€” to rise in [[Clan Khaali]], and uncover Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s killer.

Ersil played him a different tune: *The Ballad of Anthea Bloodclot* a mocking, bitter piece that unsettled Enrak. When questioned, Ersil admitted he and Anthea were childhood friends, though neither were born in Khaali. His nostalgia was laced with mystery.

Xene Redmayne soon arrived, ensuring their cups stayed full, and conversation gave way to laughter.

## 1PM â€“ Secrets Unfold

The barrel-raft bumped against the dock. Inside, nâ€™Dong was bruised, soaked, and furious. His thief had vanished the moment they landed.

Trapped, nâ€™Dong spent fifteen agonizing minutes trying to break free from within until the barrel finally tipped and he clambered out. Alone among the stolen goods, he cracked open one of the barrels and found a note: â€œHalf the load. Midnight. One Ball Innâ€ signed with a Broken Fang insignia.

A grin crept across nâ€™Dongâ€™s wet face.

He had his lead.
